<a id='question-100'></a>

### Question 100: Metronidazole Toxicity
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Metronidazole's mechanism of action against anaerobic bacteria and protozoa, and its associated adverse effects, specifically the disulfiram-like reaction and peripheral neuropathy. (甲硝唑的作用机制及其不良反应，特别是与二硫仑相似的反应和周围神经病变)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department complaining of severe nausea, vomiting, facial flushing, and headache that began approximately 2 hours after consuming a glass of wine. He reports being prescribed metronidazole 500 mg twice daily for 7 days three days ago for a suspected *Clostridioides difficile* infection, which was confirmed by PCR testing. He admits to drinking one glass of wine (approximately 5 oz) earlier today, unaware of the potential interaction. His vital signs are: Temperature 37.8°C, Heart Rate 110 bpm, Blood Pressure 130/70 mmHg, Respiratory Rate 18 breaths/min. Physical examination reveals conjunctival injection and mild tachycardia. Laboratory results show elevated liver enzymes (AST 85 U/L, ALT 95 U/L) and a slightly elevated white blood cell count (12,000/µL). The patient denies any other medication use or known allergies.

Which of the following mechanisms best explains the patient's current symptoms?

(A) Inhibition of bacterial DNA gyrase, leading to acetaldehyde accumulation.
(B) Inhibition of aldehyde dehydrogenase, leading to acetaldehyde accumulation.
(C) Inhibition of bacterial folate synthesis, leading to increased GABAergic activity.
(D) Inhibition of bacterial cell wall synthesis, leading to histamine release.
(E) Inhibition of bacterial protein synthesis via the 30S ribosomal subunit, leading to mitochondrial dysfunction.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Pharmacological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with acute onset nausea, vomiting, flushing, headache, tachycardia after drinking alcohol.
- **Medication:** Metronidazole prescribed 3 days ago for *C. difficile* infection.
- **Timing:** Symptoms started ~2 hours after alcohol consumption, while on metronidazole therapy.
- **Key Clue:** The constellation of symptoms (nausea, vomiting, flushing, headache, tachycardia) occurring shortly after alcohol ingestion in a patient taking metronidazole strongly suggests a disulfiram-like reaction.
- **Question:** Asks for the mechanism explaining these symptoms.

##### 2. Pharmacological & Molecular Deep-Dive (生化、靶点受体与机制深度剖析)
- **Metronidazole:** A nitroimidazole antibiotic and antiprotozoal agent.
- **Mechanism of Action (Antimicrobial):** Metronidazole is a prodrug activated under anaerobic conditions. Anaerobic organisms possess nitroreductases that reduce the nitro group of metronidazole. This reduction generates reactive nitro radical anions. These radicals damage bacterial DNA, leading to strand breaks and inhibition of DNA synthesis, ultimately causing cell death. It is effective against anaerobic bacteria (e.g., *Bacteroides*, *Clostridium*, *C. difficile*) and certain protozoa (e.g., *Trichomonas*, *Giardia*, *Entamoeba*).
- **Mechanism of Disulfiram-like Reaction:** Metronidazole also inhibits hepatic aldehyde dehydrogenase (ALDH), an enzyme crucial for metabolizing acetaldehyde, the toxic metabolite of ethanol. When alcohol is consumed, alcohol dehydrogenase converts ethanol to acetaldehyde. Normally, ALDH rapidly converts acetaldehyde to acetate. Inhibition of ALDH by metronidazole leads to a massive accumulation of acetaldehyde in the body. Acetaldehyde accumulation causes vasodilation (flushing), tachycardia, nausea, vomiting, headache, and palpitations – the classic symptoms of a disulfiram-like reaction. This reaction occurs when alcohol is consumed within 48 hours of starting or during metronidazole therapy.
- **Other Toxicities:** Metronidazole can also cause a metallic taste, peripheral neuropathy (dose-dependent, related to impaired pyridoxine metabolism), and dark reddish-brown urine (due to metabolites).
- **Why Option B is Correct:** Option B directly describes the mechanism of the disulfiram-like reaction caused by metronidazole: inhibition of aldehyde dehydrogenase, leading to acetaldehyde accumulation, which explains the patient's symptoms.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Inhibition of bacterial DNA gyrase is the mechanism of action for fluoroquinolones (e.g., ciprofloxacin, levofloxacin). Fluoroquinolones do not cause a disulfiram-like reaction. Acetaldehyde accumulation is not related to DNA gyrase inhibition. This option incorrectly links the mechanism to the wrong drug class and incorrectly describes the consequence.
- (C): Inhibition of bacterial folate synthesis is the mechanism of action for sulfonamides (e.g., sulfamethoxazole) and trimethoprim. This mechanism is unrelated to metronidazole or the disulfiram-like reaction. Increased GABAergic activity is associated with benzodiazepines or barbiturates, not this mechanism or drug.
- (D): Inhibition of bacterial cell wall synthesis is the mechanism of action for beta-lactams (e.g., penicillin, cephalosporins), vancomycin, and bacitracin. While some beta-lactams can cause histamine release (especially in anaphylaxis), this is not the mechanism for metronidazole's toxicity, nor does it explain the specific symptoms of a disulfiram-like reaction.
- (E): Inhibition of bacterial protein synthesis via the 30S ribosomal subunit is the mechanism of action for aminoglycosides (e.g., gentamicin, streptomycin) and tetracyclines (e.g., doxycycline, tetracycline). Mitochondrial dysfunction can be a toxicity of some antibiotics (e.g., aminoglycosides, linezolid), but it is not the mechanism underlying the disulfiram-like reaction caused by metronidazole.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Metronidazole inhibits aldehyde dehydrogenase, leading to acetaldehyde accumulation when alcohol is consumed, causing a disulfiram-like reaction (flushing, nausea, vomiting, headache, tachycardia). Remember this interaction when prescribing metronidazole. Key differential: Disulfiram itself inhibits aldehyde dehydrogenase, causing the same reaction upon alcohol consumption. Other metronidazole toxicities include peripheral neuropathy and metallic taste.

---
